0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94725pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.97221pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}
}$}}\hskip-412.8906pt\hskip 412.8906pt}\mathrm{Sp}
such that

\mathcal{A}(\alpha)\colon\mathcal{A}F\to\mathcal{A}G

is an equivalence. As
\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\mathrm{Sp})
is a full subcategory of a functor
category, equivalences are given pointwise, hence we have that, for all

n\in\mathbb{Z}

\mathcal{A}F^{n}\simeq\mathcal{A}G^{n}.

But, by Lemma
3.8

this is just means that
\alpha
induces
an equivalence on associated gradeds, hence, by
Proposition
2.21
,
it is an equivalence in
\hbox{\hskip 412.8906pt\hskip-15.45145pt\hbox{$\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}$}\hskip-15.45145pt\hskip-412.8906pt\raisebox{3.43446pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-2.33835pt}{\resizebox{48365776}{507903}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94691pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94725pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.97221pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}
}$}}\hskip-412.8906pt\hskip 412.8906pt}\mathrm{Sp}
.
∎

Lemma 3.11

There is a natural equivalence

(\mathrm{ev}_{n})_{!}\simeq(\mathrm{ev}_{n-1})_{*}

(with notation as in Proposition
2.12
) of functors

\mathrm{Sp}\to\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\mathrm{Sp})
.

Proof.

It follows from the pointwise description of Proposition
2.12

that
(\mathrm{ev}_{n})_{*}
preserves all colimits.
As
\mathrm{Sp}\simeq\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{st}}(\Delta^{0})\simeq\St(\mathcal{P}(\Delta^{0}))
,
Lemma
A.18
implies that it is enough to check that they take
the same value on
\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}\scalebox{1.25}[1.25]{\text{\char 135\relax}}_{\mathrm{pt}}\simeq\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}\mathrm{pt}\simeq\mathbb{S}
. But
this follows immediately again from description given in Proposition

2.12
.
∎

Lemma 3.12

We have equivalences

\textsc{pt}\circ\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}\scalebox{1.25}[1.25]{\text{\char 135\relax}}_{n}\simeq(\mathrm{ev}_{n})_{!}\mathbb{S}\simeq(\mathrm{ev}_{n+1})_{*}\mathbb{S}

of objects in
\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\mathrm{Sp})

Proof.

The second equivalence is an instance of Lemma

3.11
. Regarding the first one, we will show that
they represent the same functor. In fact:

\begin{split}\Map_{\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\mathrm{Sp})}((\mathrm{ev}_{n})_{!}\mathbb{S},C)&\simeq\Map_{\mathrm{Sp}}(\mathbb{S},C^{n})\\
&\simeq\Omega^{\infty}C^{n}\end{split}

and, using Proposition
A.22
:

\begin{split}\Map_{\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\mathrm{Sp})}((\textsc{pt}\circ\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}\scalebox{1.25}[1.25]{\text{\char 135\relax}}_{n},C)&\simeq\Map_{\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{st}}(\mathbf{Ch})}(\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}\scalebox{1.25}[1.25]{\text{\char 135\relax}}_{n},C)\\
&\simeq\Omega^{\infty}\map_{\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{st}}(\mathbf{Ch})}(\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}\scalebox{1.25}[1.25]{\text{\char 135\relax}}_{n},C)\\
&\simeq\Omega^{\infty}C^{n}\end{split}

concluding the proof.
∎

Notation 3.13

Motivated by Lemma
3.12
, we introduce the notation

y_{-}\mathbb{S}\coloneqq\textsc{pt}\circ\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}\scalebox{1.25}[1.25]{\text{\char 135\relax}}\colon\mathbf{Ch}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\mathrm{Sp})

to emphasize the connection with the pointed stabilization of the Yoneda
embedding.

We have the following “density” result for elements of the form

y_{n}\mathbb{S}
in
\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\mathrm{Sp})
.

Lemma 3.14

Given any
C\in\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\mathrm{Sp})
, there is a natural equivalence

C\simeq\int^{n\in\mathbf{Ch}}\map_{\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\mathrm{Sp})}(y_{n}\mathbb{S},C)\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits y_{n}\mathbb{S}

where
\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits
denotes the canonical tensoring of stable
\infty
-categories over spectra
(see
[
Lur17
, 4.8.2.20]
).

Proof.

Using Proposition
A.22

\begin{split}\Map_{\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\mathrm{Sp})}&\left(\int^{n\in\mathbf{Ch}}\map_{\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\mathrm{Sp})}(y_{n}\mathbb{S},C)\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits y_{n}\mathbb{S},D\right)\\
&\simeq\int_{n\in\mathbf{Ch}}\Map_{\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\mathrm{Sp})}\left(\map_{\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\mathrm{Sp})}(y_{n}\mathbb{S},C)\mathop{\otimes}\displaylimits y_{n}\mathbb{S},D\right)\\
&\simeq\int_{n\in\mathbf{Ch}}\Map_{\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\mathrm{Sp})}\left(\map_{\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\mathrm{Sp})}(y_{n}\mathbb{S},C),\map_{\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\mathrm{Sp})}(y_{n}\mathbb{S},D)\right)\\
&\simeq\int_{n\in\mathbf{Ch}}\Map_{\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\mathrm{Sp})}\left(\map_{\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{st}}(\mathbf{Ch})}(\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}\scalebox{1.25}[1.25]{\text{\char 135\relax}}_{n},C),\map_{\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{st}}(\mathbf{Ch})}(\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}\scalebox{1.25}[1.25]{\text{\char 135\relax}}_{n},D)\right)\\
&\simeq\int_{n\in\mathbf{Ch}}\Map_{\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\mathrm{Sp})}\left(C^{n},D^{n}\right)\\
&\simeq\int_{n\in\mathbf{Ch}}\Map_{\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{st}}(\mathbf{Ch})}\left(C^{n},D^{n}\right).\end{split}

Now, using the end formula for the space of natural transformations
(see
[
Gla16
, 2.3]
,
[
GHN17
, 5.1]
), we get that

\begin{split}\int_{n\in\mathbf{Ch}}&\Map_{\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{st}}(\mathbf{Ch})}\left(C^{n},D^{n}\right)\\
&\simeq\Map_{\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{st}}(\mathbf{Ch})}(C,D)\\
&\simeq\Map_{\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\mathrm{Sp})}(C,D)\end{split}

hence both objects corepresent the same functor.
∎

Lemma 3.15

There is a co/fiber sequence in
\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\mathrm{Sp})
given by

\mathbb{S}^{n-1}_{[n-1]}\to\mathbb{S}^{n}_{[n]}\to y_{n-1}\mathbb{S}[n]

where the first map is the structure map defined in Construction

3.5
.

Proof.

By Proposition
2.20
, the inclusion of
\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\mathrm{Sp})
into

\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{st}}(\mathbf{Ch})
preserves colimits, therefore we can understand the colimit
in the latter
\infty
-category, where it can be computed pointwise (see

[
Lur09
, 5.1.2.3]
).
The result then follows by inspection of the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{S}^{n-1}_{[n-1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{S}[n-1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{S}^{n}_{[n]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{S}[n]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y_{n-1}\mathbb{S}[n]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{S}[n]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{S}[n]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots.}

∎

The previous result lets us get an explicit description of the graded pieces
of
\mathcal{I}
.

Proposition 3.16

Let
C\in\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\mathrm{Sp})
; then

\gr^{n}(\mathcal{I}C)\simeq C^{n}[-n].

Proof.

By Lemma
3.15
, we have a co/fiber sequence

\mathbb{S}^{n}_{[n]}\to\mathbb{S}^{n+1}_{[n+1]}\to y_{n}\mathbb{S}[n+1].

By applying
\map_{\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\mathrm{Sp})}(-,C)
to it, we get

\map_{\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\mathrm{Sp})}(y_{n}\mathbb{S}[n+1],C)\to\mathcal{I}C(n+1)\to\mathcal{I}C(n),

(see Remark
3.7
)
and, by Lemma
3.12
,

\begin{split}\map_{\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\mathrm{Sp})}(y_{n}\mathbb{S}[n+1],C)&\simeq\map_{\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\mathrm{Sp})}(\textsc{pt}\circ\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}\scalebox{1.25}[1.25]{\text{\char 135\relax}}_{n},C[-n-1])\\
&\simeq\map_{\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{st}}(\mathbf{Ch})}(\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}\scalebox{1.25}[1.25]{\text{\char 135\relax}}_{n},C[-n-1])\\
&\simeq C^{n}[-n-1]\end{split}

(where the last equivalence follows from Proposition
A.22
).
∎

Corollary 3.17

Let
C\in\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\mathrm{Sp})
. Then

(1)

If
C^{m}\simeq 0
, then
\mathcal{I}C^{m}\simeq\mathcal{I}C^{m+1}
.

(2)

If there exists an
N
such that
C^{m}\simeq 0
for all
m<N
,
then
\mathcal{I}C^{m}\simeq\mathcal{I}C^{N}
for all
m\leqslant N
.

(3)

If there exists an
N
such that
C^{m}\simeq 0
for all
m\geqslant N
,
then
\mathcal{I}C^{m}\simeq\mathcal{I}C^{N}
for all
m\geqslant N
.

(4)

If there exists an
N
such that
C^{m}\simeq 0
for all
m\geqslant N
,
then
\mathcal{I}C^{m}\simeq 0
for all
m\geqslant N
and

\mathcal{I}C^{N-1}\simeq C^{N-1}[-N+1]
.

(5)

For all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have
that
\mathcal{I}\mathbb{S}^{n}_{[n]}\simeq\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}\scalebox{1.25}[1.25]{\text{\char 135\relax}}_{n}
.

Proof.

The first three points are straightforward consequences of Proposition

3.16
, whereas (
4
) follows immediately
from (
3
), together with the completeness of
\mathcal{I}C

(see Proposition
3.9
).
Point (
5
) follows from the definition of

\mathbb{S}^{n}_{[n]}
together with (
1
-
4
).
∎

Proposition 3.18

The composite
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{I}
is equivalent to
\mathrm{id}_{\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\mathrm{Sp})}
.

Proof.

It follows from Remark
3.7
that
\mathcal{I}
commutes with
arbitrary coproducts, and is thus cocontinuous. Hence,
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{I}
is
cocontinuous as well.
By Lemma
3.14
, we have that every coherent cochain
complex is canonically the colimit of elements in the essential image of

y_{-}\mathbb{S}
.
In particular, it is enough to check that the two functors agree on elements
of the form
y_{n}\mathbb{S}
.
Using Lemma
3.15
and
Corollary
3.17
.
5
, we have
that

\begin{split}\widehat{\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{I}y_{n}\mathbb{S}&\simeq\cofib\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{I}\mathbb{S}^{n}_{[n]}\to\widehat{\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{I}\mathbb{S}^{n+1}_{[n+1]}\right)\\
&\simeq\cofib\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}\scalebox{1.25}[1.25]{\text{\char 135\relax}}_{n}\to\widehat{\mathcal{A}}\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}\scalebox{1.25}[1.25]{\text{\char 135\relax}}_{n+1}\right)\\
&\simeq\cofib\left(\mathbb{S}^{n}_{[n]}\to\mathbb{S}^{n+1}_{[n+1]}\right)\\
&\simeq y_{n}\mathbb{S}\end{split}

concluding the proof.
∎

We can now prove the following theorem.

Theorem 3.19

The adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hskip 412.8906pt\hskip-15.45145pt\hbox{$\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}$}\hskip-15.45145pt\hskip-412.8906pt\raisebox{3.43446pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-2.33835pt}{\resizebox{48365776}{507903}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-188.35222pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{40911075}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-188.35222pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{40911075}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-188.3508pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{40911075}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-188.35118pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{40911075}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.97221pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}
}$}}\hskip-412.8906pt\hskip 412.8906pt}\mathrm{Sp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\mathrm{Sp}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{I}}

\dashv

is an equivalence.

Proof.

Putting together Proposition
3.18
, and Proposition

3.9
, we get that
\mathcal{A}\mathcal{I}\simeq\mathrm{id}_{\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\mathrm{Sp})}
.
This, together with (the dual of) Proposition
2.22

implies that the counit

\varepsilon\colon\mathcal{A}\mathcal{I}\Longrightarrow\mathrm{id}_{\operatorname{Ch}^{*}{\mathrm{Sp}}}
of the adjunction

\mathcal{A}\dashv\mathcal{I}
is an equivalence.
If we now consider the triangular identity

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}\mathcal{I}\mathcal{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{\mathcal{A}}}

we see that, as both
\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{A}}
and
\varepsilon_{\mathcal{A}}

are equivalences,
\mathcal{A}\eta
is an equivalence as well;
but, as by Lemma
3.10

\mathcal{A}
is
conservative, then
\eta
has to be an
equivalence, concluding the proof.
∎

We conclude the section giving more information about the functor
\mathcal{I}
;
we can leverage from Lemma
3.15
our
understanding of it, by getting
a recursive description of its components, which in turn gives a complete
description of its values in the case of bounded above cochain complexes.

In the rest of the section, we will make free use of some results about
cubic diagrams in stable
\infty
-categories, as presented in
[
DJW19
]
, and refer to

op. cit.
for all the related concepts, notations and terminology we
use and do not introduce here. The following fact is somewhat implicit in

[
DJW19
]
, but as we will use it crucially, we report it
here for convenience.

Proposition 3.20

Let
{C}
be a stable
\infty
-category, let
a\in\mathbb{N}
and let

C:(\Delta^{a})^{\mathrm{op}}\to{C}
be a functor.
Moreover, if
(\Delta^{1})^{a}
denotes the
a
-fold product of
\Delta^{1}
with
itself, and we denote by

\raisebox{3.01385pt}{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\resizebox{345886}{225735}{$\rightharpoonup$}}}v=(\raisebox{3.01385pt}{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\resizebox{345886}{225735}{$\rightharpoonup$}}}v_{a},\raisebox{3.01385pt}{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\resizebox{345886}{225735}{$\rightharpoonup$}}}v_{a-1},\ldots,\raisebox{3.01385pt}{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\resizebox{345886}{225735}{$\rightharpoonup$}}}v_{1})

the objects of
(\Delta^{1})^{a}
(where each
\raisebox{3.01385pt}{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\resizebox{345886}{225735}{$\rightharpoonup$}}}v_{i}
can either
be
0
or
1
),
let
F
be the
a
-cube having for vertices:

F(\raisebox{3.01385pt}{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\resizebox{345886}{225735}{$\rightharpoonup$}}}v)=\begin{cases}C(a)\quad&\text{if }\raisebox{3.01385pt}{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\resizebox{345886}{225735}{$\rightharpoonup$}}}v=(0,\ldots,0)\\[10.0pt]
C(a-b)\quad&\text{if }0<b\leqslant a\text{, }\raisebox{3.01385pt}{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\resizebox{345886}{225735}{$\rightharpoonup$}}}v_{i}=0\text{ for }i>b\text{ and }\raisebox{3.01385pt}{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\resizebox{345886}{225735}{$\rightharpoonup$}}}v_{i}=1\text{ for }i\leqslant b\\[10.0pt]
0&\text{else}\end{cases}

where all the nonzero maps are determined by
C

(that is,
F
is an
a
-cube having the
C(i)
’s on its
“spine” and zero objects elsewhere).
Then,

\totcof F\simeq\cofib^{a}(C)\simeq\underbrace{\cofib\kern-1.99997pt\Big(\kern-3.99994pt\cdots\kern-1.99997pt\cofib\kern-1.99997pt\Big(\kern-1.99997pt\cofib}_{a\text{ times}}\kern-1.99997pt\Big(C(a)\to C(a-1)\Big)\to C(1)\Big)\cdots\to C(0)\Big).

Proof.

By
[
DJW19
, A.24]
, one can extend
F
to a coCartesian
(a+1)
-cube

\widetilde{F}

such that
\widetilde{F}|_{\{0\}\times\Delta^{a}}\simeq F
,
and with
\widetilde{F}|_{\{1\}\times\Delta^{a}}

having
\totcof F

as its terminal vertex, and
0
elsewhere
(see
op. cit.
for a precise statement).
As
\widetilde{F}
is coCartesian, by iteratively applying
[
DJW19
, A.11]
,

\cofib^{a}(\widetilde{F})
is a coCartesian
1
-cube, i.e. an equivalence.
We conclude by observing that its source is given by
\cofib^{a}(F)
, and its
target is just
\totcof F
.
∎

Remark 3.21

By virtue of Lemma
3.7
and
Lemma
3.15
, we see that

\begin{split}\mathcal{I}C^{n}&\simeq\map(\mathbb{S}^{n}_{[n]},C)\\
&\simeq\map\Big(\fib\big(y_{n-1}\mathbb{S}[n]\to\mathbb{S}^{n-1}_{[n]}\big),C\Big)\\
&\simeq\cofib\Big(\map\big(\mathbb{S}^{n-1}_{[n]},C\big)\to\map\big(y_{n-1}\mathbb{S}[n],C\big)\Big)\\
&\simeq\cofib\Big(\map\big(\mathbb{S}^{n-1}_{[n-1]},C\big)[-1]\to C^{n-1}[-n]\Big)\\
&\simeq\cofib\Big(\map\big(\mathbb{S}^{n-1}_{[n-1]},C\big)\to C^{n-1}[-n+1]\Big)[-1]\\
&\simeq\fib\Big(\mathcal{I}C^{n-1}\to C^{n-1}[-n+1]\Big)\\
\end{split}

hence, by iteratively applying the above, we get that, for all
a\in\mathbb{N}
,

\mathcal{I}C^{n}
is naturally equivalent to

\begin{split}\overbrace{\fib\kern-1.99997pt\Big(\kern-3.99994pt\cdots\kern-1.99997pt\fib\kern-1.99997pt\Big(\kern-1.99997pt\fib}^{a\text{ times}}\kern-1.99997pt\Big(&\mathcal{I}C^{n-a}\to C^{n-a}[-n+a]\Big)\\
&\to C^{n-a+1}[-n+a-1]\Big)\cdots\to C^{n-1}[-n+1]\Big).\end{split}

By stability, the above is equivalent to

\begin{split}\overbrace{\cofib\kern-1.99997pt\Big(\kern-3.99994pt\cdots\kern-1.99997pt\cofib\kern-1.99997pt\Big(\kern-1.99997pt\cofib}^{a\text{ times}}\kern-1.99997pt\Big(&\mathcal{I}C^{n-a}\to C^{n-a}[-n+a]\Big)[-1]\\
&\to C^{n-a+1}[-n+a-1]\Big)[-1]\cdots\to C^{n-1}[-n+1]\Big)[-1]\end{split}

which, in turn, is equivalent to

\begin{split}\overbrace{\cofib\kern-1.99997pt\Big(\kern-3.99994pt\cdots\kern-1.99997pt\cofib\kern-1.99997pt\Big(\kern-1.99997pt\cofib}^{a\text{ times}}\kern-1.99997pt\Big(&\mathcal{I}C^{n-a}[-a]\to C^{n-a}[-n]\Big)\\
&\to C^{n-a+1}[-n]\Big)\cdots\to C^{n-1}[-n]\Big).\end{split}

By
3.20
,
the latter is equivalent to
the total cofiber of a suitable cube
F_{a}
, that is

\mathcal{I}C^{n}\simeq\totcof F_{a}

and hence, by
[
DJW19
, A.31]
,

\mathcal{I}C^{n}\simeq\left(\totfib F_{a}\right)[a].

We can describe
F_{a}
explicitly as follows;
if
(\Delta^{1})^{a}
denotes the
a
-fold product of
\Delta^{1}
with itself,
and we denote by

\raisebox{3.01385pt}{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\resizebox{345886}{225735}{$\rightharpoonup$}}}v=(\raisebox{3.01385pt}{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\resizebox{345886}{225735}{$\rightharpoonup$}}}v_{a-1},\raisebox{3.01385pt}{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\resizebox{345886}{225735}{$\rightharpoonup$}}}v_{a-2},\ldots,\raisebox{3.01385pt}{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\resizebox{345886}{225735}{$\rightharpoonup$}}}v_{0})

the objects of
(\Delta^{1})^{a}
(where each
\raisebox{3.01385pt}{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\resizebox{345886}{225735}{$\rightharpoonup$}}}v_{i}
can either
be
0
or
1
)

F_{a}
is the
a
-cube having for vertices:

F_{a}(\raisebox{3.01385pt}{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\resizebox{345886}{225735}{$\rightharpoonup$}}}v)=\begin{cases}\mathcal{I}C^{n-a}[-a]\quad&\text{if }\raisebox{3.01385pt}{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\resizebox{345886}{225735}{$\rightharpoonup$}}}v=(0,\ldots,0)\\[10.0pt]
C^{n-a+b}[-n]\quad&\text{if }0\leqslant b<a\text{, }\raisebox{3.01385pt}{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\resizebox{345886}{225735}{$\rightharpoonup$}}}v_{i}=0\text{ for }i>b\text{ and }\raisebox{3.01385pt}{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\resizebox{345886}{225735}{$\rightharpoonup$}}}v_{i}=1\text{ for }i\leqslant b\\[10.0pt]
0&\text{else.}\end{cases}

a=3
, the formula above looks as follows

The previous remark takes a particularly pleasant form when the complex is
bounded.

Corollary 3.22

Let
C\in\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\mathrm{Sp})
, and assume there exists an
N
such that

C^{m}\simeq 0
for all
m>N
; then for all
a>0

\begin{split}\mathcal{I}C^{N-a}\simeq\overbrace{\cofib\kern-1.99997pt\Big(\kern-3.99994pt\cdots\kern-1.99997pt\cofib\kern-1.99997pt\Big(\kern-1.99997pt\cofib}^{a\text{ times}}\kern-1.99997pt\Big(&C^{N-a}[-N]\to C^{N-a+1}[-N]\Big)\\
&\to C^{N-a+2}[-N]\Big)\cdots\to C^{N}[-N]\Big).\end{split}

Proof.

It follows from Remark
3.21
, Corollary

3.17
.
4

and
[
DJW19
, Proposition A.24]
that the
(a+1)
-cube
defined by

F(\raisebox{3.01385pt}{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\resizebox{345886}{225735}{$\rightharpoonup$}}}v)=\begin{cases}\mathcal{I}C^{N-a}[-a]\quad&\text{if }\raisebox{3.01385pt}{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\resizebox{345886}{225735}{$\rightharpoonup$}}}v=(0,\ldots,0)\\[10.0pt]
C^{N-a+b}[-N]\quad&\text{if }0\leqslant b<a\text{, }\raisebox{3.01385pt}{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\resizebox{345886}{225735}{$\rightharpoonup$}}}v_{i}=0\text{ for }i>b\text{ and }\raisebox{3.01385pt}{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\resizebox{345886}{225735}{$\rightharpoonup$}}}v_{i}=1\text{ for }i\leqslant b\\[10.0pt]
\mathcal{I}C^{N}\simeq C^{N}[-N]\quad&\text{if }\raisebox{3.01385pt}{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\resizebox{345886}{225735}{$\rightharpoonup$}}}v=(1,\ldots,1)\\[10.0pt]
0&\text{else}\end{cases}

(where
\raisebox{3.01385pt}{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\resizebox{345886}{225735}{$\rightharpoonup$}}}v=(\raisebox{3.01385pt}{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\resizebox{345886}{225735}{$\rightharpoonup$}}}v_{a},\raisebox{3.01385pt}{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\resizebox{345886}{225735}{$\rightharpoonup$}}}v_{a-1},\ldots,\raisebox{3.01385pt}{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\resizebox{345886}{225735}{$\rightharpoonup$}}}v_{0})
)
is coCartesian.
In particular, again by
[
DJW19
, Proposition A.24]
,

\mathcal{I}C^{N-a}[-a]
is the total fiber of
F|_{\Delta^{a}\times\{1\}}
;
the thesis follows from
[
DJW19
, Proposition A.31]
.
∎

The following remark, inspired by the “Gap objects” considered
in
[
Lur17
, 1.2.2]
, will be useful later, and sheds some light on the
relation between coherent cochain complexes and the objects called

\mathbb{Z}
-complexes in
op. cit.

Remark 3.24

Proposition
3.16
and the calculus of total cofibers of cubic
diagrams allow us to understand also all the intermediate subquotients of

\mathcal{I}C
.
We can consider the diagram consisting of co/Cartesian squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{I}C^{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{I}C^{n-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{I}C^{n-2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{I}C^{n-3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{I}C^{n-4}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{n-1}[-n+1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{I}C^{n-2}/\mathcal{I}C^{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{I}C^{n-3}/\mathcal{I}C^{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{I}C^{n-4}/\mathcal{I}C^{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{n-2}[-n+2]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{I}C^{n-3}/\mathcal{I}C^{n-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{I}C^{n-4}/\mathcal{I}C^{n-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{n-3}[-n+3]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{I}C^{n-4}/\mathcal{I}C^{n-2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}

from which we can deduce that, for any
n\in\mathbb{Z}

\mathcal{I}C^{n-2}/\mathcal{I}C^{n}\simeq\cofib(\partial^{n-2})[-n+1];

moreover, we can proceed inductively and apply

[
DJW19
, A.26]
to the cofiber sequences

\mathcal{I}C^{n-k+1}/\mathcal{I}C^{n}\to\mathcal{I}C^{n-k}/\mathcal{I}C^{n}\to C^{n-k}[-n+k]

to identify

\mathcal{I}C^{n-k}/\mathcal{I}C^{n}
with the total cofiber of a cube having
the truncation

\left(C^{n-k}\to C^{n-k+1}\to\cdots\to C^{n-1}\right)[-n+1]

of
C[-n+1]
on its “spine” and zeroes elsewhere; thus, by Proposition

3.20
we have

\begin{split}\mathcal{I}C^{n-k}/\mathcal{I}C^{n}\simeq\overbrace{\cofib\kern-1.99997pt\Big(\kern-3.99994pt\cdots\kern-1.99997pt\cofib\kern-1.99997pt\Big(\kern-1.99997pt\cofib}^{k-1\text{ times}}\kern-1.99997pt\Big(&C^{n-k}\to C^{n-k+1}\Big)\\
&\to C^{n-k+2}\Big)\cdots\to C^{n-1}\Big)[-n+1]\end{split}

for any
k\geqslant 2
.

4.
The general equivalence

In this section, we extend the result of Theorem
3.19

to all stable
\infty
-categories with sequential limits. Along the way, we prove that
the explicit formulas given for
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{I}
in the previous section
hold also in the general case.

Lemma 4.1

Let
{C}
be a small stable
\infty
-category. Then, the following
equivalences hold:

\Fun\left({C},\operatorname{Ch}^{*}\left(\mathrm{Sp}\right)\right)\simeq\operatorname{Ch}^{*}\left(\Fun\left({C},\mathrm{Sp}\right)\right)

and

\Fun\left({C},\hbox{\hskip Proof.

We prove this for
\operatorname{Ch}^{*}\left(\Fun\left({C},\mathrm{Sp}\right)\right)
, the
case of

\hbox{\hskip 412.8906pt\hskip-15.45145pt\hbox{$\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}$}\hskip-15.45145pt\hskip-412.8906pt\raisebox{3.43446pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-2.33835pt}{\resizebox{48365776}{507903}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94691pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94725pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.97221pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}
}$}}\hskip-412.8906pt\hskip 412.8906pt}\left(\Fun\left({C},\mathrm{Sp}\right)\right)
being entirely
analogous.
We have that, by definition of
\operatorname{Ch}^{*}\left(\mathrm{Sp}\right)
,

(8)

\Fun\left({C},\operatorname{Ch}^{*}\left(\mathrm{Sp}\right)\right)\simeq\Fun\left({C},\Fun^{0}\left(\mathbf{Ch}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Sp}\right)\right).

Now, as

\Fun\left({C},\Fun\left(\mathbf{Ch}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Sp}\right)\right)\simeq\Fun\left({C}\times\mathbf{Ch}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Sp}\right)

we have that the
\infty
-categories of (
8
)
are equivalent to the full subcategory
of
\Fun({C}\times\mathbf{Ch}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Sp})
spanned by functors that are pointed in
the second variable (that is, sending any pair

(C,0)\in{C}\times\mathbf{Ch}^{\mathrm{op}}
to
0
). Thus, as

\Fun\left({C}\times\mathbf{Ch}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Sp}\right)\simeq\Fun\left(\mathbf{Ch}^{\mathrm{op}},\Fun\left({C},\mathrm{Sp}\right)\right)

the
\infty
-categories of (
8
) are in turn equivalent to
the full subcategory

\Fun^{0}\left(\mathbf{Ch}^{\mathrm{op}},\Fun\left({C},\mathrm{Sp}\right)\right)\subset\Fun\left(\mathbf{Ch}^{\mathrm{op}},\Fun\left({C},\mathrm{Sp}\right)\right)

spanned by
pointed functors, which, by definition, is
\operatorname{Ch}^{*}\left(\Fun\left({C},\mathrm{Sp}\right)\right)
.
∎

Corollary 4.2

Let
{C}
be a small stable
\infty
-category. Then, the equivalence of
Theorem
3.19
extends to an equivalence between

\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\Fun({C},\mathrm{Sp}))
and
\hbox{\hskip Remark 4.3

One way to re-phrase Lemma
4.1
and Corollary

4.2
is to say that objects in

\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\Fun({C},\mathrm{Sp}))
can be described as bifunctors

(C,n)\mapsto G_{C}(n)
such that
G_{C}(\mathrm{pt})\simeq 0
for any
C\in{C}
,
and similarly
objects in
\hbox{\hskip 412.8906pt\hskip-15.45145pt\hbox{$\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}$}\hskip-15.45145pt\hskip-412.8906pt\raisebox{3.43446pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-2.33835pt}{\resizebox{48365776}{507903}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94691pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94725pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.97221pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}
}$}}\hskip-412.8906pt\hskip 412.8906pt}(\Fun({C},\mathrm{Sp}))
can be described as bifunctors

(C,n)\mapsto F_{C}(n)
such that
\lim_{n}F_{C}(n)\simeq 0
for any
C\in{C}
.
The equivalence of Corollary

4.2

is then given on objects by pointwise (in
{C}
) postcomposition with

\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{I}
; that is, we have

\mathcal{A}\Big((C,n)\mapsto F_{C}^{n}\Big)\simeq\Big((C,n)\mapsto\mathcal{A}\big(F_{C}^{\bullet}\big)^{n}\Big)

and

\mathcal{I}\Big((C,n)\mapsto G_{C}^{n}\Big)\simeq\Big((C,n)\mapsto\mathcal{I}\big(G_{C}^{\bullet}\big)^{n}\Big).

Remark 4.4

Let
{C}
be a small stable
\infty
-category, and let
{A}\subseteq\Fun({C},\mathrm{Sp})

be a full stable subcategory closed under sequential limits. Postcomposition
with the inclusion
\iota\colon{A}\to\Fun({C},\mathrm{Sp})
induces fully
faithful functors

\iota_{\mathrm{Fil}}\colon\hbox{\hskip 412.8906pt\hskip-15.45145pt\hbox{$\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}$}\hskip-15.45145pt\hskip-412.8906pt\raisebox{3.43446pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-2.33835pt}{\resizebox{48365776}{507903}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern and

\iota_{\mathrm{Ch}}\colon\operatorname{Ch}^{*}({A})\to\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\Fun({C},\mathrm{Sp})).

By inspection, an object
F
lies in the essential image of

\iota_{\mathrm{Fil}}
if and only if
F^{n}
lies in
{A}
for all
integers
n
, and an object
C
lies in the essential image
of
\iota_{\mathrm{Ch}}
if and only if
C^{n}
lies in
{A}
for all
integers
n
.

.

Proof.

(
1
):
Let
F\colon(C,n)\mapsto F_{C}^{n}
lie in (the essential image of)

\hbox{\hskip 412.8906pt\hskip-15.45145pt\hbox{$\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}$}\hskip-15.45145pt\hskip-412.8906pt\raisebox{3.43446pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-2.33835pt}{\resizebox{48365776}{507903}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94691pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94725pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.97221pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}
}$}}\hskip-412.8906pt\hskip 412.8906pt}({A})
.
By Lemma
3.8
together with Remark

4.3
, we have that, for any integer
m
,

(\mathcal{A}F)^{m}
is given by
\cofib\left(F_{\bullet}^{m+1}\to F_{\bullet}^{m}\right)
.
By Remark
4.4
, each
F_{\bullet}^{n}
lies in
{A}
,
hence, as
{A}
is a stable subcategory each
(\mathcal{A}A)^{m}
lies in

{A}
, and thus
\mathcal{A}F
lies in the essential image of
\operatorname{Ch}^{*}({A})
.

(
2
):
Let
G\colon(C,n)\mapsto G_{C}^{n}
lie in (the essential image of)

\operatorname{Ch}^{*}({A})
.
By Lemma
2.10
,
G
can be expressed as the
limit of bounded above complexes:

G\simeq\lim_{j\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{op}}}G^{\leqslant j};

notice that, by Remark
4.4
and by the definition of
the
G^{\leqslant j}
’s given in Construction
2.8
, all
the objects of the form
(G^{\leqslant j})^{k}_{\bullet}
belong to
{A}
.
By Remark
3.21
together with Remark
4.3
,
we have that, for any choice of integers
j
and
m
,

(\mathcal{I}G^{\leqslant j})^{m}_{\bullet}
is either zero or can be expressed as a
suitable total fiber for a finite diagram
K^{j,m}
whose entries are either
zeroes or of the form
(G^{\leqslant j})^{k}_{\bullet}[j]
for different values of
k
;
in particular,
G^{\leqslant j}
is a finite limit of a diagram with values in

{A}
.
Putting everything together (and keeping in mind that by Proposition

2.12
evaluation commutes with limits),
we have that

\begin{split}(\mathcal{I}G)^{n}&\simeq\left(\mathcal{I}(\lim_{j}G^{\leqslant j})\right)^{n}\\
&\simeq\left(\lim_{j}(\mathcal{I}G^{\leqslant j})\right)^{n}\\
&\simeq\lim_{j}(\mathcal{I}G^{\leqslant j})^{n}\\
&\simeq\lim_{j}(\totcof K^{j,n})\end{split}

is given by a sequential limit of finite colimits of elements of
{A}
,
and thus lies in
{A}
for any given
n
. This in turn proves that

\mathcal{I}G
lies in the essential image of
\hbox{\hskip 412.8906pt\hskip-15.45145pt\hbox{$\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}$}\hskip-15.45145pt\hskip-412.8906pt\raisebox{3.43446pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-2.33835pt}{\resizebox{48365776}{507903}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94691pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}
}$}}\hskip-412.8906pt\hskip 412.8906pt}({A})
.
∎

Let

under sequential limits. Then the equivalence between

\hbox{\hskip 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}
}$}}\hskip-412.8906pt\hskip 412.8906pt}({A})\simeq\operatorname{Ch}^{*}({A}).

Proof.

It follows from Lemma
4.5
that (in the
notation of Remark
4.4
)
\mathcal{A}\circ\iota_{\mathrm{Fil}}

factors through
\iota_{\mathrm{Ch}}
and
\mathcal{I}\circ\iota_{\mathrm{Ch}}

factors thorugh
\iota_{\mathrm{Fil}}
, i.e. there exist functors (which
we’ll temporarily denote
A
and
I
) such that

\mathcal{A}\circ\iota_{\mathrm{Fil}}\simeq\iota_{\mathrm{Ch}}\circ A
and
\mathcal{I}\circ\iota_{\mathrm{Ch}}\simeq\iota_{\mathrm{Fil}}\circ I
.
In particular, as

\begin{split}\iota_{\mathrm{Ch}}&\simeq\mathcal{A}\circ\mathcal{I}\circ\iota_{\mathrm{Ch}}\\
&\simeq\mathcal{A}\circ\iota_{\mathrm{Fil}}\circ I\\
&\simeq\iota_{\mathrm{Ch}}\circ A\circ I\end{split}\quad\text{ and }\quad\begin{split}\iota_{\mathrm{Fil}}&\simeq\mathcal{I}\circ\mathcal{A}\circ\iota_{\mathrm{Fil}}\\
&\simeq\mathcal{I}\circ\iota_{\mathrm{Ch}}\circ A\\
&\simeq\iota_{\mathrm{Fil}}\circ I\circ A\end{split}

A
and
I
are mutually inverse.
∎

Theorem 4.7

Let

be a stable
\infty
-category having sequential limits. Then there exists
an equivalence of stable
\infty
-categories

}$}}\hskip-412.8906pt\hskip 412.8906pt}({C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ch}^{*}({C}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Proof.

Let
U_{0}
be our universe of small sets, and let

denote a

as an element. Let us denote by

-category of

) provides a fully faithful
(see

Nik16
, Section 6]
) exact functor

The results now follows from Lemma
4.6
applied
to
\scalebox{1.25}[1.25]{\text{\char 135\relax}}^{\raisebox{-3.18884pt}{\scalebox{.7}{\kern-2.3917ptst}}}
.

\mathcal{A}F^{n}\simeq\gr^{n}F[n]

and, for

,

for a suitable cube

, given explicitly by

F_{a}(\raisebox{3.01385pt}{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\resizebox{345886}{225735}{$\rightharpoonup$}}}v)=\begin{cases}\mathcal{I}C(n-a)[-a]\quad&\text{if }\raisebox{3.01385pt}{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\resizebox{345886}{225735}{$\rightharpoonup$}}}v=(0,\ldots,0)\\[10.0pt]
C^{n-a+b}[-n]\quad&\text{if }0\leqslant b<a\text{, }\raisebox{3.01385pt}{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\resizebox{345886}{225735}{$\rightharpoonup$}}}v_{i}=0\text{ for }i>b\text{ and }\raisebox{3.01385pt}{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\resizebox{345886}{225735}{$\rightharpoonup$}}}v_{i}=1\text{ for }i\leqslant b\\[10.0pt]
0&\text{else.}\end{cases}

(see

for details about the notation).
In particular, Remark
3.24
generalizes as well,
giving

\begin{split}\mathcal{I}C^{n-k}/\mathcal{I}C^{n}\simeq\overbrace{\cofib\kern-1.99997pt\Big(\kern-3.99994pt\cdots\kern-1.99997pt\cofib\kern-1.99997pt\Big(\kern-1.99997pt\cofib}^{k-1\text{ times}}\kern-1.99997pt\Big(&C^{n-k}\to C^{n-k+1}\Big)\\
&\to C^{n-k+2}\Big)\cdots\to C^{n-1}\Big)[-n+1]\end{split}

for any
n\in\mathbb{Z}
and
k\geqslant 2
.

5.
Coherent cochain complexes and Beilinson
\mathrm{t}
-structures

In this section, we study the connection between the
pointwise t-structure on coherent cochain complexes
and the Beilinson t-structure on filtered objects and show how the
former can in some sense be interpreted as an easier to understand version of
the latter.

In fact, if
\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}{C}
is equipped with the Beilinson t-structure,
then the full subcategory of complete filtered objects inherits a t-structure
from it
7
7

7

this fact can easily be proved directly, but will be an immediate
consequence of Theorem
4.7
.
It turns out that such inherited t-structure
is equivalent to the one obtained by carrying the pointwise t-structure
on
\operatorname{Ch}^{*}{C}
along the equivalence of Theorem
4.7
;
moreover, the Beilinson t-structure on
\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}{C}

is in some sense characterized by this property and by carrying “trivial
information” on essentially constant objects
(see Theorem
5.11
for a precise statement).
In particular,
\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}{C}
and
\hbox{\hskip 412.8906pt\hskip-15.45145pt\hbox{$\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}$}\hskip-15.45145pt\hskip-412.8906pt\raisebox{3.43446pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-2.33835pt}{\resizebox{48365776}{507903}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94691pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern have the same heart (Remark

).

Definition 5.1

Let
{C}
be a stable
\infty
-category equipped with a t-structure. We define
the
pointwise t-structure
on
\operatorname{Ch}^{*}{C}
to be the one defined
by

\begin{split}(\operatorname{Ch}^{*}{C})_{\geqslant 0}&=\{C\in\operatorname{Ch}^{*}{C}\ |\ \forall n\ C^{n}\in{C}_{\geqslant 0}\}\\
(\operatorname{Ch}^{*}{C})_{\leqslant 0}&=\{C\in\operatorname{Ch}^{*}{C}\ |\ \forall n\ C^{n}\in{C}_{\leqslant 0}\}.\end{split}

We will denote the truncation functors for this t-structure by

\tau^{\text{lvl}}_{\geqslant n}
and
\tau^{\text{lvl}}_{\leqslant n}
, and the
homotopy objects by
\pi^{\text{lvl}}_{n}
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Remark 5.2

It follows immediately from the definitions that
\operatorname{Ch}^{*}{C}
has precisely
the same separatedness and completeness properties that
{C}
has.

The following definition is a straightforward generalization of the
t-structure first introduced by Beilinson in
[
Bei87
]
.

Definition 5.5

Let
{C}
be a stable
\infty
-category having all sequential limits
8
8

8

this
assumption is likely superfluous; see Remark
5.6
,
equipped with a right separated
9
9

9

unlike the previous one, this
assumption is crucial; see Remark
5.16

t-structure
({C}_{\geqslant 0},{C}_{\leqslant 0})
.

The
Beilinson t-structure

(\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}_{\geqslant 0}{C},\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}_{\leqslant 0}{C})

on
\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}{C}
is defined as follows:

\bullet

\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}_{\geqslant 0}{C}
is the full subcategory spanned by
the objects
F\in\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}{C}
such that

\gr^{i}(F)\in{C}_{\geqslant-i}\text{ for all }i.

\bullet

\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}_{\leqslant 0}{C}
is the full subcategory spanned by
the objects
F\in\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}{C}
such that

F^{i}\in{C}_{\leqslant-i}\text{ for all }i.

(Note the asymmetry in the definition). This t-structure appeared first,
in a slightly different setting, in
[
Bei87
]
.
Its existence in this generality will be a consequence
of
[
FL15
, Theorem 2.19]
together with Theorem
5.11
.
We will denote the truncation functors for this t-structure by

\tau^{B}_{\geqslant n}
and
\tau^{B}_{\leqslant n}
, and the
homotopy objects by
\pi^{B}_{n}
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Remark 5.6

The hypothesis of
{C}
having
all sequential limits in Definition
5.5
is
there just because we will use Theorem
4.7
to prove
its existence. We believe that the t-structure can exist even
without this assumption on
{C}
, but as all the examples that arise in
practice satisfy this extra hypothesis, we didn’t bother finding a proof that
does not use it.

Remark 5.7

We can infer a few properties of the Beilinson t-structure from its
definition:

(1)

Even if we are assuming
{C}
to be right separated,
\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}{C}

need not be so; in fact, the full subcategory of

\infty
-coconnective objects
\cap_{n}(\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}{C})_{\leqslant n}
consists
of all filtered objects whose associated graded is trivial, hence of
all the essentially constant objects.

(2)

Since the full subcategory of
\infty
-connective objects consists of
the levelwise
\infty
-connective ones,
\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}{C}
is left separated
if and only if
{C}
is so.

We learned the following fact from
[
BMS19
, 5.4]
; although the result in

loc. cit.
is stated in less generality, the proof carries verbatim
to the general case. We report the argument here for the reader’s convenience.

Proposition 5.8

Let
{C}
be a stable
\infty
-category having all sequential limits,
equipped with a right separated t-structure

({C}_{\geqslant 0},{C}_{\leqslant 0})
, and let
\tau_{\scalebox{.7}{$\geqslant\kern-2.45836ptn$}}
denote its
Whitehead truncation functors.
Let
\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}({C})
be equipped with the Beilinson t-structure, and
let
\tau_{\scalebox{.7}{$\geqslant\kern-2.45836ptn$}}^{B}
denote its Whitehead truncation functors.
Then, there is a natural equivalence of functors
\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}{C}\to{C}

\gr^{i}\circ\tau_{\scalebox{.7}{$\geqslant\kern-2.45836ptn$}}^{B}\simeq\tau_{\scalebox{.7}{$\geqslant\kern-2.45836ptn-i$}}\circ\gr^{i}

for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Proof.

Notice that for any
i
, the exact functor
\gr^{i}\colon\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}{C}\to{C}

carries
(\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}{C})_{\geqslant 0}
to
{C}_{\geqslant-i}
. Moreover, as by

[
Lur17
, 1.2.1.16]
each
{C}_{\geqslant-i}
is closed under extensions,
the fiber sequence

F^{i}\to\gr^{i}F\to F^{i+1}[1]

proves that
\gr^{i}F\in{C}_{\leqslant-i}
, and thus
\gr^{i}
carries
also
(\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}{C})_{\leqslant 0}
to
{C}_{\leqslant-i}
. That is, each
\gr^{i}

is
t-exact
with respect to the Beilinson t-structure on
\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}{C}

and the shifted t-structure
({C}_{\geqslant-i},{C}_{\leqslant-i})
on
{C}
.
As any exact and t-exact functor between stable
\infty
-categories equipped with
t-structures commutes with the truncation functors associated to the
t-structures, the result follows.
∎

Corollary 5.9

In particular, in the hypotheses of Proposition
5.8
,
if we denote by
\pi_{n}
the t-structure homotopy object functors of
{C}

and by
\pi_{n}^{B}
the t-structure homotopy object functors of
\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}{C}
,
we have the equivalence (natural in
F
)

\gr^{i}\pi_{n}^{B}F\simeq(\pi_{n-i}(\gr^{i}F))[-i]

for all
i,n\in\mathbb{Z}
.

We recall the following theorem from
[
FL15
]
(which is an

\infty
-categorical generalization of
[
BBD83
, 1.4.10]
).

Theorem 5.10

[
FL15
, Theorem 2.19]

Given a recollement
{C}_{0}\hookrightarrow{C}\hookleftarrow{C}_{1}
,
suppose both
{C}_{0}
and
{C}_{1}
are equipped with t-structures, then
there exists a t-structure on
{C}
, called the
glued t-structure
,
given by

\begin{split}{C}_{\geqslant 0}&=\{X\in{C}\ |\ j_{L}X\in({C}_{1})_{\geqslant 0}\text{ and }i_{L}X\in({C}_{0})_{\geqslant 0}\}\\
{C}_{\leqslant 0}&=\{X\in{C}\ |\ j_{L}X\in({C}_{1})_{\leqslant 0}\text{ and }i_{R}X\in({C}_{0})_{\leqslant 0}\}\end{split}

(with notations as in Remark
2.14
).

}$}}\hskip-412.8906pt\hskip 412.8906pt}{C}
.
We have that
F\in(\hbox{\hskip }$}}\hskip-412.8906pt\hskip 412.8906pt}{C})_{\geqslant 0}
if and only if

\mathcal{A}F\in\left(\operatorname{Ch}^{*}{C}\right)_{\geqslant 0}
.
By Definition
5.1
,
this is the case if and only if

(\mathcal{A}F)^{n}\simeq\gr^{n}F[n]\in{C}_{\geqslant 0}\text{ for all }n

hence

(\hbox{\hskip 412.8906pt\hskip-15.45145pt\hbox{$\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}$}\hskip-15.45145pt\hskip-412.8906pt\raisebox{3.43446pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-2.33835pt}{\resizebox{48365776}{507903}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern }$}}\hskip-412.8906pt\hskip 412.8906pt}{C})_{\geqslant 0}=\left\{F\in\hbox{\hskip 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94691pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94725pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.97221pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}
}$}}\hskip-412.8906pt\hskip 412.8906pt}{C}\ |\ \forall n\ \gr^{n}F\in{C}_{\geqslant-n}\right\}.

Now, according to Theorem
5.10
, the connective
objects in the glued t-structure on
\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}{C}
are given by all the

G\in\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}{C}
such that

LG\in\left(\hbox{\hskip 412.8906pt\hskip-15.45145pt\hbox{$\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}$}\hskip-15.45145pt\hskip-412.8906pt\raisebox{3.43446pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-2.33835pt}{\resizebox{48365776}{507903}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94691pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94725pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.97221pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}
}$}}\hskip-412.8906pt\hskip 412.8906pt}{C}\right)_{\geqslant 0}

(the condition on
G^{-\infty}
being empty, as we are considering the
trivial t-structure on
{C}
);
but, as for all
n
we have
\gr^{n}LG\simeq\gr^{n}G
, the above is
equivalent to the condition

\gr^{n}G\in{C}_{\geqslant-n}\text{ for all }n

which in turn determines exactly the class of connective objects for the
Beilinson t-structure of Definition
5.5
;
as, by
[
Lur17
, 1.2.1.3]
, the class of connective objects completely
determines the t-structure, provided its existence, the only thing left
is to chech is that the description of coconnective objects given in
Theorem
5.10
coincides with the one given in Definition

5.5
; that is, we have to prove that

\left(\forall n\ \gr^{n}F\in{C}_{\leqslant-n}\text{ and }F^{+\infty}\simeq 0\right)\Longleftrightarrow\left(\forall n\ F^{n}\in{C}_{\leqslant-n}\right).

For the “only if” direction:
[
Lur17
, 1.2.1.16]

implies that
{C}_{\leqslant-n}
is closed under
extensions, hence if
F^{n+1}\in{C}_{\leqslant-n-1}
and

F^{n}\in{C}_{\leqslant-n}
the fiber sequence

F^{n}\to\gr^{n}F\to F^{n+1}[1]

proves that
\gr^{n}F\in{C}_{\leqslant-n}
.
To check that
F^{+\infty}\simeq 0
, observe that any subset of the form

\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{op}}_{\geqslant n}
is an initial subcategory of
\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{op}}
, hence for any

n\in\mathbb{Z}
we have

F^{+\infty}\coloneqq\lim\left(\cdots\to F^{n+1}\to F^{n}\to\cdots\right)\simeq\lim\left(\cdots\to F^{n+1}\to F^{n}\right);

as (by
[
Lur17
, 1.2.1.6]
), each
{C}_{\leqslant n}
is closed under all limits
existing in
{C}
,

F^{+\infty}\in{C}_{\leqslant n}\ \forall n\in\mathbb{Z}

(recall that
{C}_{\leqslant m}\subseteq{C}_{\leqslant n}
for all pairs of integers

m\leqslant n
) and thus
F^{+\infty}\simeq 0
by the right separatedness
hypothesis.

For the “if” direction, let us start by noticing that as

\gr^{n+1}F[1]\in{C}_{\leqslant-n}
, the fiber
sequence

F^{n}/F^{n+2}\to\gr^{n}F\to\gr^{n+1}F

proves
F^{n}/F^{n+2}\in{C}_{\leqslant-n}
(again, as the latter
\infty
-category is closed
under limits in
{C}
). We can now proceed inductively for
m\geqslant 2
to
show that (as
\gr^{n+m}F[1]\in{C}_{\leqslant-n-m+1}\subseteq{C}_{\leqslant-n})

the fiber sequence

F^{n}/F^{n+m+1}\to F^{n}/F^{n+m}\to\gr^{n+m}F[1]

implies all objects
F^{n}/F^{k}
for
k>n
lie in
{C}_{\leqslant-n}
.
Since (again, by
[
Lur17
, 1.2.1.6]
) we know we can compute colimits in

{C}_{\leqslant-n}
just by computing them in
{C}
and then reflecting
along the left adjoint to the inclusion (in particular, the colimit
is the same in both categories if the object already happened to land
in
{C}_{\leqslant-n}
when computed in
{C}
), we have that

\begin{split}\lim_{k}F^{n}/F^{k}&\simeq\lim_{k}\cofib\left(F^{k}\to F^{n}\right)\\
&\simeq\cofib\left(\lim_{k}F^{k}\to F^{n}\right)\\
&\simeq F^{n}/F^{+\infty}\end{split}

lies in
{C}_{\leqslant-n}
. But, as by hypothesis
F^{+\infty}\simeq 0
,
we have that
F^{n}\in{C}_{\leqslant-n}
as desired.
∎

.

Remark 5.14

In the situation of Theorem
5.10
, passing to hearts one
gets a “recollement” of Abelian categories:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C}_{0}^{\heartsuit}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C}^{\heartsuit}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C}_{1}^{\heartsuit}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{L}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(j_{L})_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{L}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{R}}

\dashv

\dashv

\dashv

\dashv

where
i
,
j
and
(j_{L})_{!}
are fully faithful. As shown already in

[
BBD83
, 1.4.18]
, in this situation
j_{L}
characterizes

{C}_{1}^{\heartsuit}
as the quotient category

{C}^{\heartsuit}/{C}_{0}^{\heartsuit}
.

In particular, in the hypothesis of Theorem
5.11
, we have
that (as
{C}
is endowed with the trivial t-structure)

{C}^{\heartsuit}\simeq 0
and thus (using Remark
5.4
)

(\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}{C})^{\heartsuit}\simeq\left(\hbox{\hskip 412.8906pt\hskip-15.45145pt\hbox{$\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}$}\hskip-15.45145pt\hskip-412.8906pt\raisebox{3.43446pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-2.33835pt}{\resizebox{48365776}{507903}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern
