8.
Total homology and k-decompositions

The Postnikov tower construction makes clear how a spectrum is
uniquely determined by its homotopy groups together with its k-invariants.
In particular, any spectrum
X
uniquely determines co/fiber sequences of the
form

\tau_{[n,n+1]}X\to\pi_{n}X[n]\xrightarrow{k_{n}}\pi_{n+1}X[n+2]

for each
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, and it’s easy to verify that the
k_{n}
’s are such that

k_{n}\circ k_{n-1}[-1]\simeq 0

for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.
It is widely known among the experts
that the above observation admits a converse precisely when
the nullhomotopies for such pairwise compositions of
“truncated k-invariants” are suitably compatible
(see e.g.
[
Sag08
, Section 4]
, where an instance of this fact
is discussed for objects with finite Postnikov filtrations in the setting of
triangulated categories);
to be precise, a spectrum is uniquely determined by the datum of its homotopy
groups together with a collection of maps

\{k_{n}\colon\pi_{n}X[n]\to\pi_{n+1}X[n+2]\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
such
that all pairs of composable maps compose to a nullhomotopic one,
and all the possible higher Toda brackets are trivial.

In this section we provide a rigorous formulation of the idea discussed above
using the language of coherent cochain complexes developed in the previous
sections, generalizing it to the context of stable
\infty
-categories satisfying some
mild hypothesis
15
15

15

that is, admitting sequential limits, sequential
colimits and equipped with a right complete t-structure

and general homotopy objects in (ordinary) Abelian categories.
The process of recostructing an object from its decomposition in homotopy
objects and maps between their shifts will be given by the
total homology

construction (see
8.8
), a concept further investigated in the next
section, in relation to the spectral sequence generated by a coherent cochain
complex.

Let us begin by constructing an
\infty
-category that encapsulates
the data needed to reconstruct the objects from their homotopy groups and their
k-invariants.

Definition 8.1

Let
{C}
be a stable
\infty
-category equipped with a
t-structure.
We will denote by
\operatorname{Ch}_{{C}}^{+}({C}^{\heartsuit})
the full
subcategory of
\operatorname{Ch}^{*}{C}
spanned by those objects
C
such that

C^{n}\in{C}^{\heartsuit}[2n]
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, and refer to it as the

\infty
-category of degenerate cochain complexes
of
{C}
.

Remark 8.2

The notation
\operatorname{Ch}_{C}^{+}({C}^{\heartsuit})
is slightly abusive, as the
object depends also on the t-structure
{C}
is equipped with.

We can now construct a functor that incarnates the idea discussed in the
introduction of this section.

Remark 8.3

Let
{C}
be a stable
\infty
-category equipped with a right complete t-structure.
By the definition of right completeness, there exists an equivalence

{C}\simeq\lim\left(\cdots\to\left({C}\right)_{\geqslant n}\xrightarrow{\tau_{\geqslant n+1}}\left({C}\right)_{\geqslant n+1}\to\cdots\right).

By the right completeness of
\hbox{\hskip 412.8906pt\hskip-15.45145pt\hbox{$\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}$}\hskip-15.45145pt\hskip-412.8906pt\raisebox{3.43446pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-2.33835pt}{\resizebox{48365776}{507903}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94691pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94725pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.97221pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}
}$}}\hskip-412.8906pt\hskip 412.8906pt}{C}
(see Remark

5.2
, together with Theorem

4.7
), the fully faithful
inclusion
{C}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}({C})
factors through
the subcategory of complete objects:

\tau_{\scalebox{.7}{$\geqslant\kern-2.45836pt\scalebox{.6}{$\bigstar$}$}}\colon{C}\hookrightarrow\hbox{\hskip 412.8906pt\hskip-15.45145pt\hbox{$\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}$}\hskip-15.45145pt\hskip-412.8906pt\raisebox{3.43446pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-2.33835pt}{\resizebox{48365776}{507903}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94691pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94725pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.97221pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}
}$}}\hskip-412.8906pt\hskip 412.8906pt}({C}).

Definition 8.4

Let
{C}
be a stable presentable
\infty
-category equipped with a right complete
t-structure.
Notice that, by Remark
8.3
,
the composite
\mathcal{A}\circ\tau_{\scalebox{.7}{$\geqslant\kern-2.45836pt\scalebox{.6}{$\bigstar$}$}}\colon{C}\to\operatorname{Ch}^{*}({C})

factors through the inclusion

\operatorname{Ch}_{C}^{+}({C}^{\heartsuit})\subset\operatorname{Ch}^{*}({C})
.
We define the
k-decomposition
functor

\begin{split}\mathcal{K}\colon{C}&\to\operatorname{Ch}_{C}^{+}({C}^{\heartsuit})\\
C&\mapsto(\cdots\to\pi_{n-1}X[2n-2]\to\pi_{n}X[2n]\to\pi_{n+1}[2n+2]\to\cdots)\end{split}

to be such factorization of

\mathcal{A}\circ\tau_{\scalebox{.7}{$\geqslant\kern-2.45836pt\scalebox{.6}{$\bigstar$}$}}
through
\operatorname{Ch}_{C}^{+}({C}^{\heartsuit})
.
We refer to the differentials of the complex
\mathcal{K}C
as the

k-invariants
of
C
.

Example 8.5

Let
R
be an ordinary commutative ring, and let
\mathrm{D}(R)
denote its
derived category (considered as a stable
\infty
-category equipped with the usual
t-structure).
Given any object
X\in\mathrm{D}(R)
, its k-decomposition
\mathcal{K}X
is given
by the coherent cochain complex

\cdots\to(H^{-n+1}X)[2n-2]\to(H^{-n}X)[2n]\to(H^{-n-1})X[2n+2]\to\cdots

with
H^{-n}X\in\mathrm{Ab}
.

Example 8.6

Given any
X\in\mathrm{Sp}
, its k-decomposition
\mathcal{K}X
is given by the
coherent cochain complex

\cdots\to(\pi_{n-1}X)[2n-2]\to(\pi_{n}X)[2n]\to(\pi_{n+1}X)[2n+2]\to\cdots

with
\pi_{n}X\in\mathrm{Ab}
.
The equivalence
\operatorname{Ch}_{\mathrm{Sp}}^{+}(\mathrm{Ab})\simeq\mathrm{Sp}
can be interpreted as saying
that any spectrum can be expressed as a coherent chain complex
of (suitably shifted) Abelian groups, and, viceversa, one can construct
a spectrum from the datum of objects in
\bigoplus{Z}\mathrm{Ab}[2n]
and maps
between consecutive objects
A_{n}[2n]\to A_{n+1}[2n+2]
such that all possible
Toda brackets between them are trivial.

Remark 8.7

The previous two examples make clear how, from the point of view of
coherent chain complexes, the only difference between an object in

\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Z})
and a spectrum lies in the
\mathbb{Z}
-linearity of the k-invariants:

\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Z})\simeq\operatorname{Ch}_{\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Z})}^{+}(\mathrm{Ab})\subset\operatorname{Ch}_{\mathrm{Sp}}^{+}(\mathrm{Ab})\simeq\mathrm{Sp}.

Of course, this is just a consequence of a theorem of Shipley’s
(see
[
Shi07
]
) showing that the derived
\infty
-category
\mathrm{D}(R)
of a discrete
commutative ring
R
is equivalent to the
\infty
-category of
HR
-modules

\mathrm{Mod}_{HR}
.

We now turn our attention to the functor inverse to
\mathcal{K}
. It turns out that
the process reconstructing an object of
{C}
from its k-decomposition
is a particular case of the more general construction assigning to every
coherent cochain complex
C
the object underlying its piled-up filtration

\mathcal{I}C
.

Definition 8.8

Let
{C}
be a stable
\infty
-category with sequential limits and sequential colimits.
We define the
total homology
functor to be the composite functor

\mathcal{H}\coloneqq\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits\circ\mathcal{I}

\begin{split}\mathcal{H}\colon\operatorname{Ch}^{*}{C}&\to{C}\\
C&\mapsto(\mathcal{I}C)^{-\infty}.\end{split}

Theorem 8.9

Let
{C}
be a stable
\infty
-category with sequential limits and sequential colimits,
equipped with a right complete t-structure.
The restriction of
\mathcal{H}
to
\operatorname{Ch}_{C}^{+}({C}^{\heartsuit})

induces an equivalence

\operatorname{Ch}_{C}^{+}({C}^{\heartsuit})\xrightarrow{\sim}{C}

whose inverse is given by the k-decomposition functor of Definition

8.4
.

Proof.

By the definition of right completeness, there exists an equivalence

{C}\simeq\lim\left(\cdots\to\left({C}\right)_{\geqslant n}\xrightarrow{\tau_{\geqslant n+1}}\left({C}\right)_{\geqslant n+1}\to\cdots\right).

By the discussion in
[
Lur17
]
right before Proposition 1.2.1.17, we can
give an alternative description of the right hand side as the full
subcategory of
\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}({C})
spanned by those filtered objects for
which

(1)

For each
n\in\mathbb{Z}
,
F^{n}\in{C}_{\geqslant n}
;

(2)

For each
m\geqslant n
, the associated map
F^{m}\to F^{n}
induces an
equivalence
F^{m}\xrightarrow{\sim}\tau_{\scalebox{.7}{$\geqslant\kern-2.45836ptm$}}F^{n}
.

We first notice that, as long as (
1
) holds,
we can replace (
2
)
with the weaker assumption that

(
\spadesuit
)

For each
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, the associated map

F^{n+1}\to F^{n}
induces an equivalence

F^{n+1}\xrightarrow{\sim}\tau_{\scalebox{.7}{$\geqslant\kern-2.45836ptn+1$}}F^{n}
.

By induction, assume that for a fixed
n
and some
m\geqslant n

F^{m}\to F^{n}
induces an equivalence
F^{m}\to\tau_{\scalebox{.7}{$\geqslant\kern-2.45836ptm$}}F^{n}
. We want to prove
that
F^{m+1}\to F^{n}
induces an equivalence
F^{m+1}\to\tau_{\scalebox{.7}{$\geqslant\kern-2.45836ptm+1$}}F^{n}
.
By (
\spadesuit
),
F^{m+1}\to F^{m}

induces an equivalence
F^{m+1}\to\tau_{\scalebox{.7}{$\geqslant\kern-2.45836ptm+1$}}F^{m}
, and by our inductive
hypothesis

\tau_{\scalebox{.7}{$\geqslant\kern-2.45836ptm+1$}}F^{m}\to\tau_{\scalebox{.7}{$\geqslant\kern-2.45836ptm+1$}}\circ\tau_{\scalebox{.7}{$\geqslant\kern-2.45836ptm$}}F^{n}\simeq\tau_{\scalebox{.7}{$\geqslant\kern-2.45836ptm+1$}}F^{n}

is an equivalence, hence the composite map
F^{m+1}\to\tau_{\scalebox{.7}{$\geqslant\kern-2.45836ptm+1$}}F^{m}\to\tau_{\scalebox{.7}{$\geqslant\kern-2.45836ptm+1$}}F^{n}

(which, by (
1
), is precisely the image
under
\tau_{\scalebox{.7}{$\geqslant\kern-2.45836ptm+1$}}
of the composite
F^{m+1}\to F^{m}\to F^{n}
)
is an equivalence as desired.

As
\tau_{\scalebox{.7}{$\geqslant\kern-2.45836pt\scalebox{.6}{$\bigstar$}$}}
is fully faithful, it induces an equivalence with its
essential image in
\hbox{\hskip 412.8906pt\hskip-15.45145pt\hbox{$\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}$}\hskip-15.45145pt\hskip-412.8906pt\raisebox{3.43446pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-2.33835pt}{\resizebox{48365776}{507903}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94691pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94725pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.97221pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}
}$}}\hskip-412.8906pt\hskip 412.8906pt}({C})
(see Remark
8.3
).
As
\mathcal{A}
is an equivalence, it is sufficient to check that

\mathcal{I}\left(\operatorname{Ch}_{C}^{+}{C}^{\heartsuit}\right)\simeq\tau_{\scalebox{.7}{$\geqslant\kern-2.45836pt\scalebox{.6}{$\bigstar$}$}}{C}
.
By the definition of
\operatorname{Ch}_{C}^{+}{C}^{\heartsuit}
and Lemma

3.8
, all objects in

\mathcal{I}\left(\operatorname{Ch}_{C}^{+}{C}^{\heartsuit}\right)
satisfy
(
1
) and
(
2
).
On the other hand, again by Lemma
3.8
,
any filtered object satisfying conditions
(
1
) and
(
2
)
is such that its shelled complex lies
in
\operatorname{Ch}_{C}^{+}{C}^{\heartsuit}
. Hence,

\mathcal{I}\left(\operatorname{Ch}_{C}^{+}{C}^{\heartsuit}\right)
and

\tau_{\scalebox{.7}{$\geqslant\kern-2.45836pt\scalebox{.6}{$\bigstar$}$}}{C}
are two full subcategories of
\hbox{\hskip 412.8906pt\hskip-15.45145pt\hbox{$\mathrm{Fil}^{\text{\scalebox{0.85}{$\downarrow$}}}$}\hskip-15.45145pt\hskip-412.8906pt\raisebox{3.43446pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-2.33835pt}{\resizebox{48365776}{507903}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94691pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94725pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.97221pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}
}$}}\hskip-412.8906pt\hskip 412.8906pt}{C}
spanned
by the same objects.
To identify its inverse, it now suffices to notice that

\begin{split}\mathcal{H}\circ\mathcal{K}&\simeq\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits\circ\kern 2.5pt\mathcal{I}\circ\mathcal{A}\circ\tau_{\scalebox{.7}{$\geqslant\kern-2.45836pt\scalebox{.6}{$\bigstar$}$}}\\
&\simeq\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits\circ\kern 2.5pt\mathrm{id}\circ\tau_{\scalebox{.7}{$\geqslant\kern-2.45836pt\scalebox{.6}{$\bigstar$}$}}\\
&\simeq\mathrm{id}\end{split}

as desired.
∎

We now turn our attention to the behavior of
\mathcal{H}
on objects coming from
the (ordinary) category of chain complexes in the heart.

Proposition 8.10

Let
{C}
be as in Theorem
8.9
.
Then the t-structure homotopy objects of the total homology of an ordinary
chain complex in
C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}\in\operatorname{Ch}^{*}({C}^{\heartsuit})
(considered as an
element of
(\operatorname{Ch}^{*}{C})^{\heartsuit}
) are given by the cohomology of

C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}
:

\pi_{-n}\mathcal{H}C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}\simeq\mathrm{H}^{n}C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}.

Proof.

By Proposition
3.16
,
together with the hypothesis that
C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}
lies in

\operatorname{Ch}^{*}({C}^{\heartsuit})
, we have that

\gr^{n}\mathcal{I}C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}\simeq C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}^{n}[-n]
; in particular, (as
\mathcal{I}C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}

is complete) we see that for any integer
n
,

\mathcal{I}C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}^{n}
is
(-n)
-coconnective, and that

(15)

\pi_{m}\left(\mathcal{I}C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}^{n}\right)\cong\pi_{m}\left(\mathcal{I}C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}^{n-1}\right)\text{ for all }m\leqslant-n.

By Remark
3.24
, we have a co/fiber sequence

C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}^{n+1}[-n-1]\to\mathcal{I}C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}^{n}/\mathcal{I}C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}^{n+2}\to C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}^{n}[-n]

whose associated long exact sequence on homotopy objects lets us identify

\pi_{m}\left(\mathcal{I}C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}^{n}/\mathcal{I}C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}^{n+2}\right)\cong\begin{cases}\ker(d^{n}_{C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}})\quad&\text{ for }m=-n;\\
\coker(d^{n}_{C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}})\quad&\text{ for }m=-n-1;\\
0\quad&\text{ else.}\end{cases}

Again by Remark
3.24
,
we have a co/fiber sequence

\mathcal{I}C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}^{n}/\mathcal{I}C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}^{n+2}\to\mathcal{I}C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}^{n-1}/\mathcal{I}C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}^{n+2}\to C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}^{n-1}[-n+1]

whose associated long exact sequence starting at
-n+1
looks as follows

\cdots 0\to\pi_{-n+1}\left(\mathcal{I}C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}^{n-1}/\mathcal{I}C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}^{n+2}\right)\to C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}^{n-1}\to\ker(d^{n})\to\pi_{-n}\left(\mathcal{I}C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}^{n-1}/\mathcal{I}C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}^{n+2}\right)\to 0\cdots

letting us identify
\pi_{-n}\left(\mathcal{I}C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}^{n-1}/\mathcal{I}C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}^{n+2}\right)
as

\coker\left(C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}^{n-1}\xrightarrow{d^{n-1}}\ker(d^{n})\right)\cong\mathrm{H}^{n}C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}.

As
\mathcal{I}C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}^{n+2}
is
(-n-2)
-coconnective, the long exact sequence
associated to the co/fiber sequence

\mathcal{I}C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}^{n+2}\to\mathcal{I}C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}^{n-1}\to\mathcal{I}C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}^{n-1}/\mathcal{I}C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}^{n+2}

shows that

\pi_{m}\left(\mathcal{I}C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}^{n+2}\right)\cong\left(\pi_{m}\mathcal{I}C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}^{n-1}\right)\text{ for }m\geqslant-n-1

and thus in particular that
\pi_{-n}\left(\mathcal{I}C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}^{n-1}\right)\cong\mathrm{H}^{n}C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}
.
Finally, (
15
) implies the desired result.
∎

Remark 8.11

In the special case of
{C}=\mathrm{Sp}
, the proof of Proposition

8.10
shows that
\mathcal{I}C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}
for an ordinary chain
complex of Abelian groups
C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}
gives precisely the tower obtained through
the ‘‘brutal truncations’’
16
16

16

sometimes referred to also as the
“stupid truncation” in the literature
of the complex
C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}
. That is,

\mathcal{I}C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}^{n}\simeq H(\tau^{\geqslant n}C_{\scalebox{.6}{$\heartsuit$}}),

where
\tau^{\geqslant n}
here denotes the brutal truncation (1-)functor and
H

denotes the Eilenberg–MacLane (
\infty
-)functor
\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\mathrm{Ab})\to\mathrm{Sp}
.

In particular, we have that in the case of spectra the
total homology functor
\mathcal{H}
restricted to
\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\mathrm{Ab})\subset\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\mathrm{Sp})

coincides with the Eilenberg–MacLane functor.

We conclude the section with the following variant of Definition

8.4
.

Variant 8.12

Let
{C}
be a stable presentable
\infty
-category equipped with a right separated
t-structure, and let
q\in\mathbb{N}
be fixed.
Let
\operatorname{Ch}_{{C}}^{+q}({C}^{\heartsuit})
denote the full
subcategory of
\operatorname{Ch}^{*}{C}
spanned by those objects
C
such that

C^{n}\in{C}^{\heartsuit}[n(q+1)]
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Consider the full subcategory
{C}_{q\text{-periodic}}\subset{C}

spanned by the objects
X
such that the t-structure homotopy objects

\pi_{n}X
are isomorphic to
0
for
n
not a multiple
of
q
:

{C}_{q\text{-periodic}}\coloneqq\{X\ |\ \pi_{n}X\cong 0\text{ for }n\not\equiv 0\mod q\}.

We define
\mathcal{K}_{q}
to be the factorization of

\mathcal{A}\circ\tau_{\scalebox{.7}{$\geqslant\kern-2.45836ptq\scalebox{.6}{$\bigstar$}$}}

(where
\tau_{\scalebox{.7}{$\geqslant\kern-2.45836ptq\scalebox{.6}{$\bigstar$}$}}
denotes the sub-filtration of
\tau_{\scalebox{.7}{$\geqslant\kern-2.45836pt\scalebox{.6}{$\bigstar$}$}}

obtained by skipping all the stages of the Whitehead tower that are
not multiples of
q
)
through the inclusion

\operatorname{Ch}_{C}^{+q}({C}^{\heartsuit})\subset\operatorname{Ch}^{*}({C})
,
and refer to it as the

q
-periodic k-decomposition
functor.
Similarly to what happens for
\mathcal{K}
, the functor
\mathcal{K}_{q}
induces
an equivalence
{C}_{q\text{-periodic}}\simeq\operatorname{Ch}_{C}^{+q}({C}^{\heartsuit})
.

We learned about the following example from Achim Krause.

Example 8.13

As an instance of Variant
8.12
, we can consider
the
2(p^{n}-1)
-periodic k-decomposition of the
n
-th Morava
K-theory (
n\geqslant 1
) spectrum, for some fixed prime
p
.
As

\mathbb{F}_{p}[2m(p^{n}-1)+m]\simeq\mathbb{F}_{p}[m(p^{n}-1)]

the coherent cochain complex
\mathcal{K}_{2(p^{n}-1)}K(n)
looks as follows

\cdots\to\mathbb{F}_{p}[(m-1)(p^{n}-1)]\to\mathbb{F}_{p}[m(p^{n}-1)]\to\mathbb{F}_{p}[(m+1)(p^{n}-1)]\to\cdots

(where
\mathbb{F}_{p}[m(p^{n}-1)]
sits in degree
m
), and the differentials are
given by suitable shifts of the
n
-th Milnor primitive for the mod
p

Steenrod algebra:

\partial^{m}\simeq Q_{n}[m]\in\mathcal{A}_{p}.

9.
The spectral sequence associated to a coherent cochain complex

In this section, we discuss how coherent cochain complexes give rise to
spectral sequences.
We have the following result.

Theorem 9.1

Let
{C}
be a stable
\infty
-category with sequential limits and sequential colimits,
equipped with a right complete t-structure.
Then, every coherent cochain complex
C\in\operatorname{Ch}^{*}{C}
generates a spectral
sequence

E_{1}^{i,j}\cong\pi_{-j}C^{i}

whose
E_{1}
page is given by the homotopy groups of the components of

C
and whose
E_{1}
differentials

d_{1}^{i,j}=\pi_{-j}\partial^{i}_{C}

are obtained from the coherent differentials of
C
by passing to
homotopy.
When the spectral sequence collapses at a finite stage, it converges
strongly to the homotopy groups of the total homology of
C

E_{1}^{i,j}\cong\pi_{-j}C^{i}\Longrightarrow\pi_{-i-j}\mathcal{H}C.

We defer the proof to later in this section.
Of course, the above theorem follows at once from Theorem

4.7
together with Theorem
5.11

and the existence of the spectral sequence
associated to a filtered object in a suitable stable
\infty
-category (whose incarnation in
the case of spectra has been known by the experts for a long time and an
account of which can be found in
[
Lur17
, 1.2.2]
, in the generality of this
paper).
The goal of this section is to give a self-contained construction of the above
spectral sequence.

Remark 9.2

The convergence statement in Theorem
9.1

is far from being optimal; it is somewhat of a folk result that the
corresponding spectral sequence for a filtered object is conditionally
convergent. In forthcoming work by Hedenlund–Krause–Nikolaus, the authors
give a new proof of this fact, using the filtered counterpart of
the décalage construction we illustrate in this section using the
coherent cochain complexes perspective.

We learned about the relation between the Beilinson t-structure and Deligne’s
décalage functor from Benjamin Antieau.
Most of the ideas discussed in this section are already
present in some form in
[
Ant19
]
and
[
BMS19
, 5.5]
;
we believe that the language of coherent cochain complexes gives a
particularly pleasant perspective on the topic.

The key ingredient in the construction of the spectral sequence of a coherent
cochain complex is given by the following construction.

Definition 9.3

Let
{C}
be a stable
\infty
-category with sequential limits and sequential colimits,
equipped with a right separated t-structure, and
let
C\in\operatorname{Ch}^{*}{C}
be a coherent cochain complex. We can apply the functor

\mathcal{K}
levelwise, to obtain a bicomplex (denoted
\mathcal{K}^{\text{lvl}}C
)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{K}C^{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{K}C^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{K}C^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{0}C^{0}[0]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{0}C^{1}[0]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{0}C^{2}[0]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}C^{0}[2]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}C^{1}[2]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}C^{2}[2]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}C^{0}[4]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}C^{1}[4]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}C^{2}[4]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}

=

=

=

i.e. such that
(\mathcal{K}^{\text{lvl}}C)^{i,j}\simeq\pi_{j}C^{i}[2j]
,
where the horizontal differentials are the k-invariants for the relevant
objects, and the vertical ones are induced from the differentials of
C
.
We define the

décalée complex
17
17

17

our choice of terminology
is justified by the construction of the spectral sequence of
Theorem
9.1
(see the proof at the end of the section):
the functor
\dec
provides an
incarnation in coherent chain complexes of Deligne’s

décalée
filtration (see
[
Del71
, 1.3]
)

denoted
\dec C
to be the levelwise
total homology of the bicomplex
\mathcal{K}^{\text{lvl}}C
with respect to the
horizontal maps of the above diagram.
That is,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dec C^{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{H}\bigg(\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{0}C^{0}[0]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{0}C^{1}[0]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{0}C^{2}[0]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots\bigg)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dec C^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{H}\bigg(\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}C^{0}[2]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}C^{1}[2]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}C^{2}[2]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots\bigg)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dec C^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{H}\bigg(\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}C^{0}[4]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}C^{1}[4]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}C^{2}[4]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots\bigg)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}

or, more precisely:

\dec\coloneqq\operatorname{Ch}^{*}(\mathcal{H})\circ\mathcal{K}^{\text{lvl}}.

As
\dec
gives an endofunctor for
\operatorname{Ch}^{*}({C})
, we will denote
by

\dec^{n}\colon\operatorname{Ch}^{*}({C})\to\operatorname{Ch}^{*}({C}),\text{ with }n\geqslant 1

its iterations.
We will also use the convention
\dec^{0}\coloneqq\mathrm{id}
.

Remark 9.4

In the situation of Definition
9.3
, using
Remark
5.15
together with
Remark
8.11
we see that (omitting as usual
the Eilenberg–Maclane functor from the notations)

\begin{split}(\dec C)^{n}&\simeq\mathcal{H}\left(\left(\pi_{n}^{\text{lvl}}C\right)[2n]\right)\\
&\simeq\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits\mathcal{I}\left(\left(\pi_{n}^{\text{lvl}}C\right)[2n]\right)\\
&\simeq\left(\mathop{\operatorname{colim}}\displaylimits\mathcal{I}\left(\pi_{n}^{\text{lvl}}C\right)\right)[2n]\\
&\simeq\left(\mathcal{H}\left(\pi_{n}^{\text{lvl}}C\right)\right)[2n]\\
&\simeq\left(\pi_{n}^{\text{lvl}}C\right)[2n]\\
&\simeq\left(\pi_{n}^{B}\mathcal{I}C\right)[2n]\\
\end{split}

that is,
\dec C^{n}
is equivalent (up to a shift) to the
n
-th Beilinson
homotopy chain complex of the piled up filtered object of
C
.

Remark 9.5

In the situation of Definition
9.3
,
we have that

\mathcal{H}C\simeq\mathcal{H}\dec C

for any
C\in\operatorname{Ch}^{*}{C}
, and thus

\mathcal{H}C\simeq\mathcal{H}\dec^{n}C

for any
n\in\mathbb{N}

commutes).
It follows from Theorem
4.7
that given any

}$}}\hskip-412.8906pt\hskip 412.8906pt}({C})
, we have an equivalence of bifiltered objects

\tau^{B}_{\geqslant\scalebox{.6}{$\bigstar$}}F\simeq\mathcal{I}\mathcal{A}\tau^{B}_{\geqslant\scalebox{.6}{$\bigstar$}}F\simeq\mathcal{I}\left(\pi^{B}_{*}F[2*]\right)\quad\in\hbox{\hskip
